# Draft 26 of Agreements: the trace of §0 to §3

*A working file for the companion, by the scribe's session M2, 27 November 2026. Not a decision, and not part of the hashed text. One line per sentence of §0 to §2 of `draft-26.md` and one per definition of its §3: what the line rests on, his words where they reach it (quoted exactly, typos as the ledger has them), and a mark. The companion's sessions fold it in.*

**The wish** (Nobody, allegedly, 26 November, 16:46, at the top of `docs/decisions.md` on main): "We need to change the current wish to updating the core after the findings". On what carries over (16:47): "Yes, we’ll go back to analysis, maybe add the focus on agreements". On earlier answers (26 November, 16:03): "Yes, but me saying something does not mean that something was necessarily a good idea".

**Marks.** **TRACED**: to a rule or a ledger entry, cited. **COUNTED**: by a script, named. **INFERRED**: this session's reading. *The ledger* is `docs/decisions.md` on main, entries cited by heading and date. *D25* is draft 25 (`claude/agreements-clean-1-hahuwn`, pull request 183), its section and bullet. *The outline* and *the brief* are pull requests 292 and 289. *SN* is `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-settled-by-the-lead.md`, *CN* the changes note. *The lead's readings* are the MOR lead's readings of 27 November given to this session (on the brief's items 1, 4, 28 and the other unsaid items; on G3.1, G6.1/G6.2, G13.1, G5.6(f)/G10.6(a), G4.9/G4.11(i); on the outline's rows): they are the lead's, not his, and are marked so; none is yet in the ledger, so none is cited to it. *Cross-references* to §4 to §7 follow the outline's layout of draft 26 (its section 3); M7 closes them.

## §3 Definitions

*Stays* means D25's words, with "place" read as keeper, cross-references moved to draft 26's sections, and history and attributions taken out ("Only rules in the hashed text", 17 Nov; and the assignment: his words are quoted in the trace, never in the spec text). ### 3.5 What relays do

## Counts

COUNTED, `words.py` (this session's script, below; it counts 88 bold head words, since "act id" stands beside Fingerprint and "late" beside Late update, which the outline counts as one): of the outline's 90 words and phrases, **86 are defined** in §3, each once, in the group the outline gives it; **4 are not** (delegation, goes; triggered and join, history; rejected, not an answer word). **No word was added.** "Act id" (3.1, under Fingerprint) and "stored" (3.5) are named inside definitions the outline lists, not as words of their own; both are listed here so M11 can judge.

```
# words.py: count the bold head words of each §3 bullet against the outline's 90
import re,sys
t=open(sys.argv[1]).read()
s=t[t.index('## 3. Definitions'):t.index('## 4. ')]
heads=[re.findall(r'\*\*([^*]+)\*\*',l.split(':')[0]) for l in s.splitlines() if l.startswith('- **')]
print(sum(len(h) for h in heads))
```

Each word they reach is defined as far as the sources agree. Items 3 and 18 are answered by the lead's readings (G3.1; G5.6(f)/G10.6(a)), and item 19 by SN M15, which the outline follows.

## Rows to check

1. **§8.** The lead's reading puts the thirteen rules moving to the MICS in "§8" of draft 26; the outline lays out §0 to §7 only, and the assignment asks for headings of §4 to §7 alone. §2 names §8; no §8 heading is written. The MOR lead to say where they sit.
2. **"Where Rely is".** §0 says knots' importance "is taken up where this text treats relying on acts". If N10 stays out of draft 26, the only place is §5.13 (`relies_on`); the outline's reading (ii) would then point outside the text.
3. **The cases of invalid.** D25 lists them, in order, inside its definition. Draft 26's definition points to §4 and §5 instead, since its cases are changing (G3.1), held (G6.1), moving (G14.3) or new (N9). A later session (M3 or M7) writes the ordered list, in §4 as the outline's "one fault, one rule" has it, or back in §3.
4. **Succession** is listed in the outline as D25's "stays", but D25's entry is a history note like Triggered and Join, which the outline sends to the companion. Followed: kept, as "not a term of this text", without its history.
5. **"A keeper's record"** stands in the lead's line for "shown after" and is not defined. Identity draft 16's "a named keeper recorded it" (rule 34) reads draft 24's keeper record, which draft 26 has not (the lead's reading on item 4). The two may be one thing or two.
6. **"Uploader".** He says "An uploader lodges"; Envelopes draft 12 defines an uploader as a bake's signer. Draft 26's "lodge" says "whoever puts an act at a relay (an uploader)", which is wider than Envelopes' word. For Envelopes, when it takes the three verbs.
7. **The §5 subheadings.** The placeholder lists §4.1 to §4.3 and §5.1 to §5.13 as the outline names them, so that the definitions' cross-references land; the assignment named §4 to §7 only.
8. **"Not shown" and a missing act.** D25 has "not shown: … an act not held might" and, since "Knots are knots", a named act not held is unchecked. The definition reads D25's case as an act named by none held. If the MOR lead reads it otherwise, the two definitions need one more look.
9. **The blind reader** (a subagent shown §0 and §3 only) could answer all three questions. Fixed after its reading: a relay "vouching for nothing" against a keeper that vouches (3.5 now says how); the answers' list said which word is of what; "withdrawal" written as a counter signature; "the order the Terms need" reworded; the baker named as Envelopes' word. Not fixed, since each would need a choice: which Terms fix the known parties (item 2); how several keepers give one order (item 5); "a keeper's record" (row 5); anchoring's "nothing is later" beside "never absence" (D25's words, kept); whether an agreement named as a party that has not signed has a say in updates; D25's "own parties", "Control; the Money layer", "without bytes attached" and the spot kinds, kept as D25 has them.

## M3: rows to check

1. **Which fields may hold the set of acts.** The assignment asks §4 to say which. No ledger entry or note names a field. The impact sheet's fifth vector puts one in `relies_on`, but there an entry is read as a knot, and a set is "no knot by itself" (SN M21). §4.1 says no field of the tables holds one and points to §7. M7 carries it there. The MOR lead to say.
2. **An agreement as author.** D25's G3.1 allows signers or roles, and encoding 2's E13 makes an author naming an agreement invalid. M2's Author, and 5.3.1 written in its words, allow an agreement. A Terms act carries no `for` or `under`, so how an agreement signs Terms as their author is not said. The lead's "a named party" does not settle which reading is meant. For M8 (E13) and the MOR lead.
3. **`envelope`, `envelope_at` and G14.3.** The fields stay and their rules move (outline row 8). 5.1.4 and case 7 of the list point to "Envelopes' rules for Terms that name an envelope", which Envelopes draft 12 does not yet have.
4. **The place of cases 7 and 8** in "One fault, one rule": D25's order is kept, and N9 is placed last. Added by M3, to check.
5. **The report paragraph** of §4.3 is kept, though the outline does not list it.
6. **5.1.2's "nothing about me" sentence** applies the 18 November rule to an agreement's parties. G5.1(b) (§5.6) carries it in general; M5 should word it so the two agree.

## M3: for M2's words

1. **"text act" and "Text act".** §3.1 (The words) writes "the text act", and §2 writes "The text act (rule 14)". §4 and §5.2 write "Text act", as the ledger does. The blind reader noticed the difference.
2. **Author: "who signs them".** One Terms act has one signer, but `authors` may name several, and §3.1 Terms says "signed by its author". Whether every author signs the Terms act, or one signs it and the others assent, is not said (see left out).
3. **Call: "A call names no place".** "place" here is D25's word, not the keeper. §4.2 `calls` copies M2's wording. Whether "place" stays is M2's or M11's to judge.

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given §3, §4.2 and §5.3 alone. It answered all three questions:

- Authors: parties named as signers or as agreements, never by role (85% sure).
- Invalid: an author named by role, or one who is not among the parties (70% sure that this is the whole list, since §5's other sections were not given).

Fixed after its reading:

- `authors` now says how an author is matched to a party: by position.

Not fixed, since each would need a choice or M2's words:

## M4: left out, by the brief's item number

- **Item 2** (which Terms "when made" fix the known parties; a party a later version adds): 5.5.5(a) and 5.5.7(e) say "the known parties (§3.2)" and nothing about which version. D25's "a party that a version took out is not asked by a later update" goes with it.
- **Item 5** (several keepers: does one silent keeper stop the pin?): 5.4.1, 5.5.1(b) and 5.5.5(d) say only "agree" and "disagree".
- **Item 7** (how a fork that is not of a line, a completing assent against a counter signature, is resolved in bytes): 5.4.5 says "as §5.12 says".
- **Item 10** (whether a resolving update must itself be pinned): 5.5.7(g) says nothing.
- **Item 11** (a filling whose version's words state no term): 5.4.3 and 5.5.7(f) say "for as long as its words say".
- **Item 12** (which act "the anchoring of the purchase" anchors): not written in §5.5; §5.10's (M6).
- **Item 13** (what a verifier shows of an agreement dissolved among the known parties while a filling's version still binds): 5.5.6 keeps D25's "MUST NOT answer … ended" and says nothing more.
- **Item 24** ("rejected"): not used; the answer for a late update is **late**.
- **Item 29** (the line's "close the offer, or dissolve it among themselves"): not written.
- **Item 35** (the two copies of the anchor entry): 5.5.7(a) and (c) point to §5.8, which M5 writes.
- **Left out for Nobody, allegedly** (carried, as the assignment asks, to the end of the stage): whether the Terms act's signer must be among its `authors`. Nothing in §5.4 or §5.5 decides it.
- **Whether a counter signature cancels once a fork with the coming into force is resolved** (the 18 November line "Carla's withdrawal stands" against the 27 November line "Neither takes effect"): 5.4.5 says only that neither takes effect while the fork stands.
- **Whether an agreement named as a party, which has never signed, is among the default signers of an update** (M2's blind reader's point): 5.5.5(a) says only that such a party signs as its own Terms require.

## M4: rows to check

1. **D25's "in force at once" clause** (G4.1: Terms naming no party as a signer, with no `in_force`) has no case once every author is named as a signer (edit (d)); it is not written. If the lead's Author reading changes, it comes back.
2. **A counter signature as a pin.** By knots, the pin of a first version is an act by each needed party shown after every needed assent ("In force once pinned"). A counter signature by such a party, shown after those assents, would then both pin the Terms and come after their pinning. 5.4.1 says a counter signature of those Terms is not an act that pins: **added by M4, to check**. Without it, 5.4.1 and 5.4.5 contradict each other.
3. **5.4.2 and updates.** D25's G4.2 asks every Terms act that names a role to carry `in_force`; an update's `in_force` is left unread (SN Q7; 5.2.3). Written as D25 has it, so an update with roles must carry a field nobody reads. SN M11 says the role's module (`in_force`) is read "for who fills a spot", while §4.2 gives each role its own spot socket (K5) for that. The MOR lead to say whether 5.4.2 reaches updates.
4. **"Neither takes effect"** (5.4.5) is the lead's "Arrived at" line on "A fork is a fork", not his words; the 18 November line it refines said "Carla's withdrawal stands". Written only for while the fork stands.
5. **"Its order after the last replacement".** The entry's wording (the MOR lead's) is read in 5.5.1(b) as two parts: shown after the acts that put the named version in force, and every rival that passes the gate shown after it ("the pin asking when, against the others"). In 5.5.4 the test for a late L uses only the first part of F's pin, since the second asks that L be shown after F, which is what is being tested. INFERRED; the MOR lead to confirm.
6. **The set of acts and `continues`** (edit (e)). `resolves` now holds the set's value, and the set "names no act a verifier can read without the list it was computed from" (§4.1). §4.3 still asks that `continues` be "one of the entries of `resolves`", and §4.3's "What a resolving update carries" and §5.12 read the tips from `resolves`: from a set's value alone neither can be checked, and the tips are no longer knots of the resolving update. Where the list travels, and how `continues` is checked against it, is unsaid. For M7 (§5.12) and M8 (encoding 3), and the MOR lead.
7. **"Binds the known parties to them"** (5.4.3) is "binds the seller to her" written in this text's words; in an agreement whose known parties are not all sellers, the two may differ. INFERRED.

2. **§4.2, the `calls` row**: "A call names no place" copies the old Call definition; edit (c) changed the definition only.
3. **§4.2, the `authors` row, and case 5 of the one-fault list**: both still allow an author "named as a signer or as an agreement"; edit (d) narrowed §3.2 and 5.3.1 only. They now disagree with 5.3.1.
4. **§4.2, the `resolves` row**: still "fingerprints of every tip of a fork"; edit (e) changed §4.3's row only, while §4.1 now names `resolves` as the set's field (see rows to check, 6).
5. **§3.3, Pin**: "its order after the last replacement of the version it names" is read by 5.5.1(b) as above (rows to check, 5); the definition may want the same two parts.
6. **§3.3, Late update**: "shown after the version that replaced it" is written in 5.5.4 as "shown after O was replaced by F" (after acts enough for F to replace O), since the Terms act F alone may be held before it replaced anything.

## M4: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given §3 and §5.5 alone, and the case: version 1 signed by two known parties, naming keeper K and no update condition, with a buyer who filled a role under it; version 2 signed by the two known parties only, lodged at K and shown there after version 1's coming into force; then version 3, naming version 1, shown by K later than version 2. Its answers:
1. Version 2 is in force: **valid** "by default" (5.5.7(e)), pinned per K (5.5.1(b), 5.5.5(d)), qualified **per keeper**, K named (85% sure).
2. The buyer keeps her filling under version 1. She is not asked to sign version 2 (5.5.5(a)), and version 2 does not reach what she bought (§3.2 Unknown party; 5.5.7). She is 70% sure, because §5.10 was not given.
3. Version 3 is a **late update** (5.5.4): it replaces nothing, and is answered **late**, per keeper (80% sure).

Fixed after its reading: 5.5.1(b) read as circular (version 2's pin asks that version 3 be shown after it, and version 3 is late only once version 2 has replaced version 1). It now says the order between two updates is read from the acts that put each through the gate, as 5.5.4 says, not from either update's own pin.

Not fixed, since each needs a choice or another session's words:
- §3.3's Late update ("shown after the version that replaced it", the Terms act) and 5.5.4(b) (the position at which each passes the gate) give two tests. Listed under "For M2's and M3's words", 6.
- Whether the author's signature on the Terms act counts as their assent (§3.1: "stating Terms is not agreeing to them"). Left out, with the author question carried for Nobody, allegedly.
- What of the version in force reaches a filling's holder's later acts (receiver, powers, keepers), and whether she stays a party if a later version drops her role: §5.10's (M6).
- Whether a late update is also answered **valid** by the version it names (5.5.4(d) hands that to the condition).
- "Signer" used both for a party named as a signer and for anyone who signs (M2's §3).

# M5: the trace of §5.6 to §5.9

One line per rule M5 wrote, then the edits the MOR lead asked of §2 to §5.5. Marks and abbreviations are M2's. His words are quoted as the ledger on main has them, typos included.*

## M5: left out, by the brief's item number

- **Item 5** (carried from M4; several keepers: does one silent keeper stop the order?): 5.8.3 says only "agree" and "disagree".
- **Item 6** (what a keeper answers beyond order): 5.8.4 is written for order only.
- **Item 8** (which acts compete for the same state; whether an agent's act against the end of its power is a fork): 5.6.4 says the act is not shown as binding where nothing orders it, and that whether it is a fork is not said by that rule.
- **Item 12** (carried from M4; which act "the anchoring of the purchase" anchors): 5.8.5 says only that how long a filling lasts is counted per clock; §5.10's (M6).
- **Item 18** (whether an assent made for her under a power she gave counts for G10.6(a)): 5.6.6 writes "a filling is governed by the version its proof names, which no update reaches" and nothing on how she may consent to a change; M6's. 5.6.5(f) is the lead's reading for an assent to an update, not for a filling.
- **Item 30** (the line's "Terms state their anchors (a keeper, a clock to anchor to, or both)" as a rule for all Terms): not written; 5.8.6 reaches only a cMIP that needs a clock or a keeper.
- **Item 34(a)** (how Terms name their clock, against the 17 November cut of G7): 5.8.5 uses the `clock` field as §4.2 has it. **Item 34(b)** (where a cMIP's declared needs are written): 5.8.6 says "a cMIP that needs a clock or a keeper" and not how that need is read.
- **Item 35** (carried from M4; the two copies of the anchor entry): not used. Only the first copy stands on main; its "Arrived at" also withdraws "The MOR lead's "not shown" … for such conditions", which item 35 (ii) lists as a difference, so 5.8.6 says "refused, not answered" and does not say what replaces "not shown" for such Terms beyond invalid.
- **Whether a cMIP that needs a clock, in Terms that name a keeper and no clock (or the reverse), makes them invalid**: found by M5; 5.8.6 says "neither a clock nor a keeper", and no more (rows to check, 6).
- **The lead's readings to list** (as asked): the agreement named as a party that never signed is a known party and a default signer (5.5.5(a), edit (h)); the act `body` names, held and not a Text act (M4's list, carried). **Left out for Nobody, allegedly** (carried to the end of the stage): whether the Terms act's signer must be among its `authors`.

## M5: rows to check

1. **"Never by an update one did not sign."** The assignment's plain words for §5.6 say one is bound "by an update one signed; never by an update one did not sign ("Nothing about me without my signature")". The ledger's entry of that name is about powers ("a power to act for someone counts only where that person's own signature is on the version that gives it"); the brief and the outline keep G5.1(c), changed: a known party bound by an update she did not sign, through the update condition of Terms she is bound by, once it passes the gate and the pin. 5.6.1(c) is written as the brief and outline lay it out; read the other way, (c) and 5.6.2's second way go, and a two-of-three update condition binds no third party. The MOR lead to say which. INFERRED.
2. **Knots where the Terms name keepers.** 5.8.4 (D25's G7.2) counts an act for order "only once those keepers show it"; 5.4.1 and 5.5.1(b) give the pin "by the keepers … or by knots", and 5.5.4(c) lets knots show L late "whatever a keeper shows". Written as each source has it; whether knots alone pin, where the Terms name keepers that do not show the acts, is unsaid ("Knots are knots": "in ordering any knot that can be leveraged should be leveraged", against "The place is a pointer": "from it and from nothing else"). INFERRED.
3. **The window of a counter signature** (edit (e)). By the lead's row 2, a counter signature shown after the completing assents is late, whether or not the other parties have yet pinned; so 5.4.5 now closes the window at the completion shown, not at the pin, which D25 and M4 had. The keepers' reading in 5.4.5 ("after the position at which the Terms came into force, the pin included") is left as M4 wrote it: with keepers the two positions are one. INFERRED.
4. **The late-update test** (edit (g)). 5.5.4's lead sentence now says "through O's gate and pin"; (a) still asks only the pin's first part of F (shown after the acts that put O in force), since its second part (L shown after F) is what is being tested. Kept as M4 wrote it. INFERRED.
5. **5.7.3 and the E23 sentence in 5.7.1** are added by M5, to check: the first sets the lead's reading out as a sentence of the text; the second writes encoding 2's test of "the same agreement" into the rule it serves. Either may belong to the companion or encoding 3 instead. INFERRED.
6. **"Naming neither"** (5.8.6): the entry says "a cMIP named in the Terms that needs a clock or a keeper, the Terms naming neither". Read literally, Terms that name a keeper and no clock are not invalid under N9 even where the cMIP needs a clock; another reading asks for what the cMIP needs. Written literally. INFERRED.
7. **"Grantor"** (5.6.6) is a word D25 defines inside its rule, not in §3. Kept as D25 has it; M11's check of "each word defined once" may move it. INFERRED.

## M5: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given M2's §3 and M5's §5.6 and §5.8 alone, and the case: Terms in force name two keepers and no clock; P gave A a power to sign for her; the two keepers give different orders for two acts; the words say "open for one month". Its answers:
1. P is bound by what A signed, under 5.6.1(b), where P's own signature is on the version that gives the power and the power's rule says yes; unchecked if the rule's cMIP is lacking (75% sure). Whether A is bound, it could not say (40%).
2. About the order: **not shown**, the keepers the Terms name disagreeing, both named, each one's records shown as evidence (5.8.3, §3.6) (85%).
3. About the month: no answer of these grammar rules; the time is only in the words, which they do not read (5.8.6); a reader's own clock gives only that reader's answer (5.8.5) (70%).

Fixed after its reading: 5.8.4 now says that, with several keepers, an act counts at a position only where they agree (5.8.3); 5.8.6 now says what a verifier gives where only the words speak of a time (no answer of these grammar rules).

Not fixed, since each needs a choice or another session's words:
- Whether the agent who signs for another is bound by that act: no rule of D25 or the ledger says (5.6.1(a) and 5.6.3 speak of the signer's own name only).
- Whether an author's signature on the Terms act counts as their assent (§3.1: "stating Terms is not agreeing to them"); carried with the author question for Nobody, allegedly.
- Knots against keepers (rows to check, 2).
- Whether two acts that the keepers order differently are a fork (item 8; §5.12, M7's).
- Whether a keeper alone is a stated anchor for a condition in time (rows to check, 6; §3.4 makes the keeper an anchoring for order only).
- Whether a verifier passes on the body cMIP's (K1) answer about a time: §3.6 lists K2, K3, K6, K8 and K5 as tasks whose yes or no is passed on, not K1 (M2's words).

## M6: rows to check

1. **5.4.2 sends "who fills a role" to §5.10, and §5.10 reads no `in_force`.** 5.10.2 (D25's G10.2) has the K5 cMIP of each role say who fills it; SN M11 calls `in_force` "the role's module", read "for who fills a spot". Edit (a) names §5.10 among `in_force`'s readers as asked; no sentence of §5.10 reads it. The MOR lead to say whether K2 or K5 says who fills. INFERRED.
2. **A filling read from its own version** (5.10.2, added after the blind reader). D25's "The role and its cMIP are read from the Terms in force" left a filling under a version that drops its role with nothing to read it by, against G10.5's "a filling already earned stays a filling, shown with its proof". The addition reads it from its own version, "whatever a later version says of the role"; this may say more than his words on "her spot" (item 29). INFERRED.
3. **"Filling a spot does not bind any other party"** (D25's G10.3) against 5.4.3 and N5 ("binds the known parties to them"). Kept, with a clause added that the version binds them. INFERRED.
4. **Which clock.** The assignment and the outline say "the clock the Terms name"; 5.4.3 and 5.5.7(f) (M4's) say "the clock that version names". 5.10.6 and 5.10.8 follow M4. Where a later version names another clock, the two differ; the blind reader asked which one. INFERRED.
5. **G11.2 and G11.7 hold sentences on how a report is read** (a report "listed … ties nothing"; matched, mismatched, one side only; "rejected" for a report naming no call; "never received on the buyer's word alone"). The outline keeps both "as draft 25 has it"; the assignment says "Nothing of how a report is read is written here", and the lead's reading on G11.3 sends such sentences to the MICS. Cut, and listed for the companion's "for the MICS". INFERRED.
6. **N6 and G10.7 in one rule** (5.10.8), and N5 apart from G10.6 (5.10.6, 5.10.7). The outline lists them apart; merged where they overlap. INFERRED.
7. **Which keepers qualify a filling** (5.10.2: "per keeper or as shown"): those of the Terms in force, or of the version its proof names; the blind reader asked. Not written. INFERRED.

## M6: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given M2's §3, M4's §5.5 and M6's §5.10 alone, and the case: Terms in force name a clock; Ana fills a role under version 1, whose words give her a month; the known parties sign version 2, in force, which drops her role; three weeks have passed on the clock. Its answers:
1. Ana holds a filling, tied to its proof and to version 1, with exactly version 1's rights, for a month on version 1's clock from the anchoring of the purchase; whether it still applies is the K1 cMIP's answer, shown per clock, unchecked without it; version 2 does not reach it (5.10.6, 5.10.7, 5.10.8) (80%).
2. The known parties are bound to her by version 1, for as long as its words say, whether or not a later version is in force; version 2 releases nothing (5.10.3, 5.10.6) (70%).
3. Her spot under version 2: for new identities, version 2 has no role, so nothing to ask (or unchecked); her filling stays a filling, shown with its proof, version 1 named, no later version named as changing it (5.10.5, 5.10.7) (45%).

Fixed after its reading: 5.10.2 now reads a filling already earned from the version its proof names, whatever a later version says of the role (its first point: under version 2 "nothing can find or show Ana's filling"); 5.10.6 now says the version binds the holder "by the assent that earned the filling (5.4.3)" (its point on "bound only by their own assent").

Not fixed, since each needs a choice or another session's words:
- Whether a spot still exists once a version drops its role, and what a verifier answers on new identities then ("asks nothing" or unchecked): item 29.
- What "the anchoring of the purchase" starts from, and for a filling not bought: item 12.
- Which clock, where version 1 and the Terms in force name different ones (rows to check, 4), and which keepers (rows to check, 7).

## M7: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given the whole of `draft-26.md` and the case: version 1 in force, no keeper, no update condition; U1 and U2, each naming version 1, each signed by every known party, each passing the gate, neither naming the other; then a Statement of kind `resolve` by one known party naming both, U1 continuing; a buyer filled a role under version 1 before. Its answers:
1. **After the two updates**: a fork of Terms; both tips stop; version 1 stays in force; the verifier answers **fork**, shows both, chooses neither, **as shown** (5.5.1, 5.5.4(e), 5.12.2, 5.12.3) (80%).
2. **The resolve**: it is an Agreements act but resolves nothing: a fork of Terms is resolved only by a Terms act naming version 1 in `replaces`, signed by default by every known party; a Statement of kind `resolve` is for a signer's own line; no power to resolve is given; the verifier answers **not shown** as a resolving update, and the fork stands (5.12.4, 5.12.5, 5.12.7, §4.3) (70%).
3. **The buyer**: version 1 in force, with the fork shown, as shown; her filling **shown** with its proof, version 1 named; no update reaches it; whether her rights still apply is the K1 cMIP's answer, passed on (5.10.2, 5.10.6, 5.10.7, 5.10.8) (75%).

Its five cross-references (it checked six: §4.2's `in_force` row to 5.2.3; 5.5.1(b) to 5.5.5(d); 5.8.6 to the one-fault list's case 8; 5.12.5 to 5.5.7(g); 5.4.2 to 5.2.3 and 5.5.1; §6's opening to §7): all six point where they should.

Fixed after its reading: it could not tell which reason 5.12.4 gives the resolve Statement ("nothing to resolve" or "not signed as this rule asks"). 5.12.4 now says "nothing to resolve" where what `resolves` names are not the tips of a fork in the act's own line (for a Statement of kind `resolve`, its signer's line; for a Terms act, the agreement's chain).

Not fixed, since each needs a choice or another session's words: a signer's own line forked by assents to two tips (rows to check, 4); 5.5.1(b)'s colon, the two "The buyer's leg" heads, and a version that names no clock (for earlier words, 2 to 4); whether the resolve Statement is itself a tip in its signer's line, which turns on its `previous` (the case did not give it).

## M8: Open (byte choices nobody has made; listed, none picked)

1. **The set's label**: the tag text for `tagged_hash` (draft 26, §7). INFERRED: a new tag would also be named in Identity's list of the core's tags.
2. **How a set's members are joined**: (a) the 32-byte ids concatenated (the MOR lead's "joined", in the exchange); (b) a deterministic CBOR array. The empty set's bytes follow.
3. **Where the set's rule stands**: a row of E5 (i), or an E-rule of its own, E26 (ii) (the impact sheet gives both).
4. **Which fields may hold a set's value**: none in encoding 3, as draft 26 §7 says ("in this text none does"); the impact sheet's fifth vector (a set's value in `relies_on`) is the codemeister's choice, to check.
5. **The keeper field's form**: (i) always a list, `[+ hash]`; (ii) `hash` for one, `[2* hash]` for several.
6. **Whether the keeper list is sorted** (a set, E18) or keeps the writer's order.
7. **What a keeper's identifier is the hash of**: (a) its identity hash; (b) opaque 32 bytes until the layer that orders acts gives one.
8. **Whether key 9 stands** as 32 bytes (item 34(a); draft 26 §7, "How Terms name their clock").
9. **A `words` act held and not opened, or not held**: a note (unchecked) or nothing (not read) (SN Q24 may answer it: the outline's row 11).
10. **Which spec hashes are `TEXT`**: Text draft 11's alone, or any Text draft's.
11. **Case 8 (5.8.6)**: how a cMIP's declared needs are read from bytes, and by whom: (a) handed to the checker beside the acts, in a form Development would give; (b) the grammar verifier's alone (item 34(b); draft 26 §7).
12. **`SPEC_DRAFT_26`**: not a choice; computed at M11 once the text is final.

## M8: For the text (for M11: rules of draft 26 the bytes cannot check)

1. **"In this text none does" (§4.1, §7) cannot be checked from bytes.** A set's value and an act id are both 32 bytes; a set's value written in `relies_on` or `answers` passes as an act id not held, and under 5.13.3 a chain that needs it is unchecked. Draft 26 may want to say how such an entry is read. INFERRED.
2. **`replaces` "in every Terms act except the first" (§4.2)** cannot be checked from one act: any Terms act without `replaces` reads as a first, the root of an agreement of its own. Encoding 3 says so (E19); the text may want the same sentence. INFERRED.
3. **Case 8 (5.8.6)** cannot be checked from the act's bytes: no field of Terms says what a cMIP declares. §4.3 places it among the cases a verifier "names" in order; until §7's open value is fixed, no checker of bytes gives it. INFERRED.
4. **5.2.2's "held and not a Text act"** leaves a held act that cannot be opened unsaid (encoding 3 reads it as not invalid, by §3.6's last test). INFERRED.
5. **§4.2's `calls` gives a call's form to the MICS's block**, while encoding 3 keeps encoding 2's `[task, from]`: until the MICS gives a call's bytes, the bytes stand without a text that gives them. INFERRED.
6. **§4.2's `keeper` row** says "each by the identifier the layer that orders acts gives it": no layer gives one, so E6's keeper preimage cannot be fixed from the text. TRACED to 5.8.1.

```
# rules9.py: the draft 25 numbers and N numbers that section 5 gives an entry, against the 43 + 9
import re,sys
t=open(sys.argv[1]).read(); s=t[t.index('## 5. '):t.index('## 6. ')]
want='G1.2 G1.4 G3.1 G4.1 G4.3 G4.10 G4.5 G4.6 G4.7 G4.7(a) G4.7(b) G4.7(c) G4.8 G4.8(a) G4.8(d) G4.9 G4.11(a) G4.11(c) G4.11(d) G4.11(e) G4.11(f) G4.11(g) G4.11(i) G5.1(b) G5.1(c) G5.2 G5.4 G5.6(a) G5.7 G5.7(a) G5.7(b) G5.7(c) G7.1 G7.2 G7.4 G10.5 G10.6 G10.7 G12.1 G12.3 G13.1 G13.3 G13.4'.split()+['N%d'%i for i in range(1,10)]
import re as r
miss=[w for w in want if not r.search(r'[( ]'+r.escape(w)+r'[,);]',s)]
print(len(want),'wanted;',len(miss),'missing',miss)
```

## M9: for the text (for M11)

Where explaining a rule showed it cannot be understood from draft 26 alone. Nothing changed in `draft-26.md`.

1. **Which clock counts a filling's term.** 5.8.5 says that where the Terms in force name a clock, what time it was is answered per that clock "for every answer that turns on a time, … how long what a filling gave lasts (5.4.3, §5.10) among them". §3.3 Apply, 5.4.3, 5.5.7(f) and 5.10.8 say the clock that version names, "never that of the Terms in force" (the lead's reading of 13:20). The two disagree where a later version names another clock. 5.10.5's "per the clock the Terms name" does not say which Terms (the blind reader).
2. **Which keepers qualify a filling.** 5.10.2 answers each filling "per keeper or as shown (§5.8)", and 5.8.4 takes the keepers of the Terms in force; the 13:20 reading says "a filling's clock and keepers are its own version's". The text does not say so for keepers (M6's rows to check, 7, carried).
3. **"An anchoring" in "shown after".** §3.4 and 5.13.3 say B is shown after A by "a keeper's record or an anchoring that places them so", unqualified; 5.5.3 and 5.13.5 say a reader's own anchoring never puts Terms in force or makes an update late. Read alone, 5.13.3 lets any anchoring show "after" for the pin.
4. **§0's "where this text treats relying on acts".** With N10 outside the text, the pointer lands on 5.13.2 ("An act names what it relied on"), which states a rule and does not take up knots' importance (M2's rows to check, 2, carried). The companion gives the importance (section 5, under 5.13.3).
5. **5.10.6 beside 5.10.7.** "For a filling, no later update exists" reads as absolute; 5.10.7 asks a verifier to name "each later version that changes what it earned", by the holder's own assent. The text does not say that her own assent to a later version is not an update reaching her (the blind reader).

## M10: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment was given section 8 and draft 26's §5.5, §5.7 and §5.12, and asked to work scenario 8.7 (the double assent) and 8.4 (two keepers) step by step from the rules; then three rows of section 9 drawn at random (seed 1127: 5.13.1, 5.5.5, 5.8.3), with draft 26 and this trace. Its answers: every step of both scenarios agrees with the stated answers (65% to 85% sure); row 5.13.1 matches; rows 5.5.5 and 5.8.3 match in part.

Fixed after its reading: 8.4's variant named "a fourth known party" that its act (1) does not have (rewritten: an update condition that asks any one of the three); 8.4 and 8.7 now give the condition's answer word, **not valid**, on the assents counted; 8.7's **shown** for the resolve is marked the scribe's word; row 5.5.5 lacked "Valid and in force, two words, two jobs" (the script now matches a heading's short form before a comma); row 5.8.3 lacked G7.2 (its last sentence, for one keeper, kept in 5.8.3).

Not fixed, since each needs a rule's words or a reading (For M11, 1, 2, 4, 5): whether a named condition's cMIP is given the forked assents; which assent counts for a gate after her line is resolved; the answer word for a resolving update that resolves; "rival" undefined. Also: which acts pin an update by knots needs 5.13.3 and 5.4.1, outside what it was given.

## M11: the blind reader

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor the assignment read `draft-26.md` alone, once. Its answers (70% to 85% sure): the text is a grammar by which a verifier tells, from signed acts alone, who is bound by which Terms, the rules people agree by left to cMIPs in eight sockets; two people write a Text act with the words, a Terms act (`parties`, `authors`, `body`, `previous`) and an assent each, and the first version is in force once its order is shown, by keepers or by knots; a change is a Terms act naming the old one in `replaces`, which replaces it only through the gate and the pin, two valid updates nothing orders being a fork; someone who joins later keeps the rights of the version their proof names, on its clock, which no update reaches.

The five sentences it understood least: §2's MICS line (rules kept in a companion that is not the text); §3.1 **Vow** ("without bytes attached"); 5.4.1's pin by knots (one long sentence; a counter signature that pins); 5.10.8's "anchoring of the purchase"; 5.11.7's payment holding K6 against §3.1's **Task** (a field naming a cMIP). Fixed: 5.4.1's sentence split in three, its words unchanged. Not fixed, each a choice: the other four, and the counter signature that pins (the lead's 09:47 reading).

3. 8.24: an offer V1 whose words renew "on the terms then current"; Ana fills its role; V2 replaces V1; the month ends on the clock. Rule 5.10.7. Ana's filling **shown** under V1, no later version named; with her new assent to V2, a second filling **shown** under V2.

5. 8.26: Ben assents to V1 for Ana under a power she signed. Rule 5.6.3. Ana **shown** as bound by V1; Ben **not shown**, "made for another under a power".

## N1: the sixteen definitions said again

M11's list names seventeen terms in eleven rules (COUNTED: line, update, fork, shared act, tip, branch; sequence, leg, doer, receiving side; late update; update condition; the original; set aside; buyer's contest; receiver for the act; the sorts of a spot). Each rule now keeps the words it needs and cites the §3 entry; each definition stays as §3 has it. Each row: the sentence that went, and the one that stays.

| Rule | Went | Stays |
|---|---|---|
| 5.12.2 line, update, fork, shared act, tip, branch | - **5.12.2** **Lines, updates and forks.** A **line** is a set of acts of which each names the one before it, so that only one act may follow each: one signer's acts within an agreement, by `previous` (5.7.1); an agreement's Terms, by `replaces` (§5.5); and any other acts these grammar rules read as following one another. An **update** of an act is an act that names it as the one before, and that all the parties that needed to sign it have signed: for one signer's line, that signer (5.7.1); for Terms, those whose signing the update condition asks, or the default (the gate, 5.5.1(a), 5.5.5, 5.5.7). For Terms, a late update (5.5.4) is no update of the version it names. Where an act has one update, that update is the next act of the line. A **fork** is two acts competing to change the same state, with no order shown between them: two updates of the same act of one line (5.5.4, 5.7.2); Terms coming into force against a counter signature that cancels one of the assents they need (5.4.5); or any other. Three or more are one fork with that many tips. Two acts of one signer that both name no `previous` are a fork with no shared act. These grammar rules see a fork from the acts alone: for a fork of a line, two acts were signed by all the parties that needed to sign them. They say nothing of what anyone meant. Where the fork is of a line, the act both follow is the **shared act**; each of the two updates is a **tip**; a tip, with every act that follows it in the line, is a **branch**. | - **5.12.2** **Lines, updates and forks.** A line, an update, a fork, and a fork's shared act, tips and branches, are as §3.3 defines them (**Line**, **Update**, **Fork**): for one signer's line, by `previous` (5.7.1); for an agreement's Terms, by `replaces` (§5.5), the gate deciding which signatures an update needs (5.5.1(a), 5.5.5, 5.5.7); for Terms, a late update is no update of the version it names (5.5.4). Only one act may follow each act of a line: where an act has one update, that update is the next act of the line. Two acts of one signer that both name no `previous` are a fork with no shared act. For a fork of a line, each of the two acts was signed by all the parties that needed to sign it. |
| 5.11.1 sequence, leg, doer, receiving side | - **5.11.1** **Sequence, and leg.** Where what one agreement gives or owes reaches another agreement (a party named as an agreement, 5.1.1; a receiver that is an agreement, 5.1.3), and so on from one to the next, those agreements are a **sequence**. A **leg** is one obligation carried out, between its two sides: the **doer**, who carries it out, and the **receiving side**, who receives it. A payment to an agreement is a leg between the payer and that agreement's receiver for the payment (5.1.4). A payout divided among several is one leg for each of them: between whoever pays out (a service, or a party who receives for the others) and each one paid; where one paid is an agreement, that agreement's receiver for the act (5.1.4). These grammar rules define the two sides of a leg, and nothing more of it. Each leg | - **5.11.1** **Sequence, and leg.** A sequence, a leg, and a leg's doer and receiving side are as §3.3 defines them (**Sequence**, **Leg**, **Doer**, **receiving side**). What one agreement gives or owes reaches another through a party named as an agreement (5.1.1) or a receiver that is an agreement (5.1.3); a payment to an agreement, or a payout to one, is a leg whose receiving side is that agreement's receiver for the act (5.1.4); whoever pays out a payout divided among several is a service, or a party who receives for the others. Each leg |
| 5.5.4 late update (the first mention) | two updates, each allowed by the version they name. A late update (below) is no update, and no tip. **A late update.** | two updates, each allowed by the version they name. **A late update.** |
| 5.5.4 late update | L is a **late update** where L is shown after the acts that put F, the version L should have named, through O's gate and pin (5.5.1), as (a) and (b) say. A late update replaces nothing, and is no tip of a fork: O stays replaced by F, and the line goes on from F as if L were not there. Where nothing shows it, L and F are a fork whose shared act is O, | L is a late update (§3.3, **Late update**) where L is shown after the acts that put F, the version L should have named, through O's gate and pin (5.5.1), as (a) and (b) say: O stays replaced by F, and the line goes on from F as if L were not there. Where nothing shows it, L and F are a fork whose shared act is O, |
| 5.5.7 update condition | with its parameters: their **update condition**, called the condition for short. It is one simple field, and these grammar rules read one thing of it: whether the agreement can be updated or not. Asked of a proposed update, that is **valid** or **not valid**, as that cMIP answers: the gate (5.5.1). Everything else is that cMIP's to specify: who must sign an update (two owners of three, one named party, everyone), which parts of an agreement an update may change (a share, a party, the rights a version offers, the offer itself, a power, the judge, the condition itself), when, and on what. It is never asked about a filling: no update reaches one (§5.10). | with its parameters: their update condition (§3.3, **Condition**). It is one simple field, and these grammar rules read one thing of it: asked of a proposed update, **valid** or **not valid**, as that cMIP answers: the gate (5.5.1). What that cMIP specifies includes who must sign an update (two owners of three, one named party, everyone), which parts of an agreement an update may change (a share, a party, the rights a version offers, the offer itself, a power, the judge, the condition itself), when, and on what. |
| 5.5.5 the original | A Terms act replaces the Terms its `replaces` names (the **original**) only as 5.5.1 says | A Terms act replaces the Terms its `replaces` names, the original (§3.3, **Version**), only as 5.5.1 says |
| 5.12.4 set aside | Every other tip is **set aside**: it never counted as the next act of the line (for Terms, it never counted as the next version), and no act that follows it in the line counts. | Every other tip is set aside (§3.3, **Set aside**), and no act that follows it in the line counts. |
| 5.11.8 buyer's contest | **A buyer's contest** is a contest by which a buyer says that what they bought was not delivered after payment. It is made against the offer or vow the buyer bought under, | **A buyer's contest** (§3.1, **Contest**) is made against the offer or vow the buyer bought under, |
| 5.11.8 buyer's contest, only there | These grammar rules read a buyer's contest only there: where what was bought was not delivered after payment. | These grammar rules read a buyer's contest for that case only. |
| 5.1.4 receiver for the act | it gives or owes to that agreement's **receiver for the act**: the `receiver` named by the version of that agreement which the act that gives or owes names (§5.13). | it gives or owes to that agreement's receiver for the act (§3.2, **Receiver for an act**), read from the version of that agreement that the act names (§5.13). |
| 5.10.1 the sorts |   - its **sort**, one of: **done** (whoever proves they have done X), **received** (whoever proves they have received Y), **holding** (whoever proves they are holding Z), **fits** (whoever proves their identity fits the conditions in W), or **future**; |   - its sort (§3.2, **Spot**), one of: done (whoever proves they have done X), received (whoever proves they have received Y), holding (whoever proves they are holding Z), fits (whoever proves their identity fits the conditions in W), or future; |
| 5.5.7 everything else kept | What that cMIP specifies includes who must sign an update | Everything else is that cMIP's to specify: who must sign an update |

Marks: each TRACED to §3 (the entry cited); that nothing a rule needed was lost is INFERRED (rows to check, 6). Not in M11's list and not touched: 5.11.8's "A contest is an act of these grammar rules" against §3.1 **Contest**; 5.1.3 against §4.2's `receiver` row; 5.4.4 against 5.4.5; 5.4.1's and 5.5.3's "Which keepers" (the stranger's finds, below).

## N1: the stranger

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor this wave's assignment read `draft-26.md` alone, once, from a copy in scratch space. Its four answers: (1) a grammar by which a verifier tells, from copies of acts alone, who is bound by which Terms, eight tasks left to cMIPs, "not to be used bare" (90%); (2) a Text act, a Terms act (`parties`, `authors` naming at least the first writer, `body`, `previous`), an assent each, and the pin, by keepers or by each party acting again after the assents (70%); (3) a change passes the gate and the pin; two that nothing orders are a fork, both stop, the last shared version stays; a late one replaces nothing; a resolving update ends a fork (80%); (4) someone who joins through a role keeps exactly what the version their proof names gave, on its clock, no update reaching them, changed only by their own assent (85%). Each matches the text (INFERRED).

The five sentences it understood least: 5.10.8's "anchoring of the purchase" (item 12, remains); 5.12.2's sentence on the signatures of a fork of a line (N1's own wording: fixed); 5.4.2's "read for who fills a role" (remains); 5.11.9's contest naming an envelope while §4.3 gives a Statement no `envelope` field (remains); 5.4.5's counter signatures that "lean on one another" (remains).

Its errors: fixed, needing no choice: §3.6's "(a fingerprint where §4 says fingerprint)"; 5.5.7(a)'s pointer "(5.12.2)", where tips stop in 5.12.3 (the lead's 15:34 words cite 5.12.2, and stay quoted so in the companion); "rule" alone for a grammar rule in 5.8.6 and 5.6.6. Listed, each a choice: §4.4's heading and "one fault fits two rules" (encoding 3 quotes the heading 18 times); "Control" both a layer and task K6; a payment holding K6 against §3.1 **Task** (M11's list too); §3.1 **Rule** "trusts nobody" against K3's trust column; **sort** used for a spot and a power (the lead's lean gives both the word); 5.1.3 against §4.2's `receiver` row, 5.4.1 against 5.5.3 ("Which keepers"), 5.4.4 against 5.4.5, sentences said twice beyond the sixteen; §4.1's "is open (§7)" beside §7's "in this text none does".

# P1: wave five, the text: his three decisions, the lead's readings, the wording splits

*Added by the scribe's session P1, 27 November 2026, the first of three of wave five. Not a decision, and not part of the hashed text. Nothing above is changed. Marks are M2's: TRACED (to a rule or a ledger entry, cited), COUNTED (by a script, named), INFERRED (P1's reading). The scripts are P1's scratch scripts, outside the repository: `edits.py` holds every edit, `apply.py` applies them to `draft-26.md` as N1 left it (commit 1f81c2a5), asserting each old wording occurs exactly once, and logs each; `xref.py` is the references check (M7's, written again: every "§" reference and every rule number, with its lettered clause, against the headings and rules that exist). His words are quoted as the ledger on main has them, typos included; the readings note is `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md` on main, every row the lead's, not his. Only `draft-26.md` and this trace are touched; encoding 3, the companion and the report are P2's and P3's.*

**The wish** (Nobody, allegedly, 26 November, 16:46): "We need to change the current wish to updating the core after the findings". On the order (20 November, 22:58): "Yes, all the work carries. But first the findings and the problems then the changes, the review and the verifications." And (27 November, 14:53): "We finish this rewrite of agreements, then we turn our attention to money, and after that we’ll tackle development."

**In plain words.** Three things he decided this evening are now rules: a buyer's assent gives nobody a power over her; known parties and unknown parties are two classes with two sets of rules, said in the introduction and at the head of the definitions; an update everyone needed signs, each after the other, is ordered by their own acts, so a keeper that dies can be left. Thirty-nine of the lead's fifty readings went into the rule each touches; four are encoding 3's alone, left to P2; seven change no words. Of Fable's ten splits, seven now have one answer from the text, one is P2's, and two are listed. What still needs a choice is listed below.

| P1-20 A3 / B C3,F4 | 5.5.1(b) |   - (b) **The pin.** Its order after the last replacement of that version is shown, by the keepers that version names (5.5.5(d)), or, where it names none, by knots (§5.13); where it names keepers, only they pin, and an act they do not yet show is not yet placed (§3.4). It is shown after them where it, or an act that put it through the gate, is. What shows the order depends on who signed. An update signed by every signer it needs, each shown after the other, is pinned by their knots (§5.13), whether or not that version names a keeper. An update signed by fewer than all it needs is pinned by every keeper that version names (5.5.5(d), 5.8.3), or is not shown as pinned; where that version names none, by knots. ## P1: For Nobody, allegedly

One line each; nothing chosen.

1. The rules of each class (§3.2 **Two classes**, P1-3) are the lead's line of 18:47; his words say only that they are "not bound by the same rules". To check.
2. A known party who fills a spot of its own version: P1 wrote the line, "the known parties' rules apply to it", so an update it is bound by as a known party reaches that filling (5.6.1(c)); whether the rights of 5.10.7 still stand for it is not said.
3. Which signers an update "needs" under a named condition (every party it counts, or a set enough for the gate; the lead's "a majority's update" is among those a keeper orders), and how "each shown after the other" reads for three signers or more (5.5.1(b)).
4. Whether a first version every party signs, each after the other, is pinned by their knots whatever keepers it names (5.4.1 keeps "only they pin"; the entry speaks of updates).
5. With keepers named and F pinned by its signers' knots, whether knots alone show a rival L late (5.5.4(a) still says "only as a named keeper's contradiction").
6. Fable 1.1: does a Terms act count as its author's assent for 5.4.1 and 5.5.7(e) ("the rest adds signature later"); the note has no row.
7. A Terms act signed by one who is not among its parties: an author (5.3.1, "whether or not `authors` names them") who is no party (§3.2 **Author**); Fable 1.10, scenario 8.25.
8. Which act of a filling's holder "gives that power in an act of her own" (5.6.1(b), 5.10.7), powers being given by known parties.
9. Fable 1.10: a counter signature in a party's own name against an agent's assent for her; the pin of a party named as an agreement (one act `for` it, or one signing).
10. Where the principle stands: the line of 16:05 says "stated in the Agreements companion"; the reading F18 says the introduction; P1 wrote it in §0 (P1-1) as the assignment asks.
11. N1's wordings the note does not reach (seven) and N1's lines it does not reach (fifteen), as listed above.
12. 5.6.1(b)'s "Terms ... which are in force" beside 5.6.4's act made before a replacement (the stranger; P1 changed neither).
13. Fable's question 5, second half: what shows that a signer's fork was made after the update came into force (5.12.3's new sentence assumes it is shown).

# P2: wave five, the bytes and the companion: encoding 3 and the companion brought to draft 26 as P1 left it

*Added by the scribe's session P2, 27 November 2026, the second of three of wave five. Not a decision, and not part of the hashed text. Nothing above is changed. Marks are M2's: TRACED (to a rule or a ledger entry, cited), COUNTED (by a script, named), INFERRED (P2's reading). The scripts are P2's scratch scripts, outside the repository: `enc.py` holds every edit to `encoding-3.md` and asserts each old wording occurs exactly once; `compA.py` to `compF.py` the same for `draft-26-companion.md` (A: the head, the quotations, section 3; B: section 5; C: section 6; D1 and D2: section 8; E: section 9; F: the last section); `quotes.py` finds every quotation in a file that stands in draft 26 as N1 left it (commit `1f81c2a5`) and not in draft 26 as P1 left it (commit `f7d7c1ce`); `xref_enc.py` checks every rule number and lettered clause of draft 26 cited in a file against the rules that exist, and every quotation against draft 26's words. His words are quoted as the ledger on main has them, typos included. The readings notes are `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md` (the fifty rows) and `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-readings-for-p2.md` (the readings of 20:41 and 20:55), both on main, every line the lead's. `draft-26.md` and the report are not touched.*

**The wish** (Nobody, allegedly, 26 November, 16:46): "We need to change the current wish to updating the core after the findings". On the order (20 November, 22:58): "Yes, all the work carries. But first the findings and the problems then the changes, the review and the verifications." And (27 November, 14:53): "We finish this rewrite of agreements, then we turn our attention to money, and after that we’ll tackle development."

**In plain words.** The rules changed this evening; the bytes and the companion now follow them. In the bytes: one form for the keeper field, a sorted list; the clock kept; no grammar field for a set's value, and its label, joining and empty set left to the codemeister before any vector; every Text draft listed as `TEXT`; `refs` and `prev` read as knots; the signer the first author, unlisted; a signer who is no party invalid, on the lead's reading; the words note named by 5.2.2; an empty keeper list E18's fault. In the companion: every quotation of a changed rule brought up; his three decisions with his words; the lead's fifty readings, the 18:41 reading and those of 20:41; ten new scenarios and eighteen corrected; the trace tables' rows; and the 26 rules that moved, in one table. What could not be carried without a choice is listed below.

## P2: questions for the MOR lead, one batch

Each with the scribe's lean; none is his.

1. Reading 1 says the needed signers are "those the condition's cMIP requires (K2's output)". The update condition is K4 (§6); K2 is `in_force`. Is K4's output meant? *Lean*: K4, the update condition's.
2. An update signed by every signer it needs, whose acts are not (yet) shown each after the other: may the keepers named still pin it (5.5.1(b), 5.5.5(d) "neither it nor the acts its gate counts wait on those keepers")? *Lean*: yes: knots give a second way, not the only one, so a keeper's order still pins it (scenarios 8.6, 8.17).
3. Reading 1 against the lead's own turn in "A dead keeper", which counts "a majority's update" among what a keeper orders: under a named condition that accepts two of three, is an update the two sign "signed by every signer it needs"? *Lean*: the reading's words make it so; the turn's example then needs re-reading (scenario 8.28's variant). This one may be his: it decides whether a named condition can let a minority of known parties leave a keeper.
4. Reading 5, "a Statement in her own name, naming the grantee and the power's sort": is the Statement of a kind of its own (a K7 kind), or any Statement whose `content` says so? *Lean*: a K7 kind, so that K3 reads it by its cMIP (scenario 8.34).
5. Reading 6 for a resolving update, which carries no `parties`: is its signer asked to be a party of the shared Terms? *Lean*: no test from its bytes; the gate already asks who signs it.

# P3: wave five, the closing pass: the words for the readings of 20:41, 20:55 and 21:45 and his answers of 20:50 and 21:42; the whole check; the hash

*Added by the scribe's session P3, 27 November 2026, the last of three of wave five. Not a decision, and not part of the hashed text. Nothing above is changed. Marks are M2's: TRACED (to a rule or a ledger entry, cited), COUNTED (by a script, named), INFERRED (P3's reading). The scripts are P3's scratch scripts, outside the repository: `edits.py` holds every edit to `draft-26.md` and `apply.py` applies them to the file as P1 left it (commit `f7d7c1ce`), asserting each old wording occurs exactly once; `comp.py` and `enc.py` do the same for the companion and encoding 3 as P2 left them (commit `d68ca7a`); `xref.py`, `words.py`, `quotes.py`, `rulequotes.py` and `hiswords.py` are the checks below. His words are quoted as the ledger on main has them, typos included. The notes are `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md` and `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-readings-for-p2.md` on main, every line the lead's.*

**The wish** (Nobody, allegedly, 26 November, 16:46): "We need to change the current wish to updating the core after the findings". On the order (20 November, 22:58): "Yes, all the work carries. But first the findings and the problems then the changes, the review and the verifications." And (27 November, 14:53): "We finish this rewrite of agreements, then we turn our attention to money, and after that we’ll tackle development."

**In plain words.** The last readings and his two answers of the evening are now rules. Publishing the Terms is its signer's yes. An update every known party signs, each after the other, is ordered by their own acts; any other update, two of three included, waits on every keeper named, and a dead keeper is left only by a new version every known party signs. With keepers named, acts naming acts still show an update late. A buyer can give a power only by a Statement of her own. A signer who is no party makes the Terms invalid. Then the three files were checked again as one, a stranger read the rules alone, and the hash was computed last.

## P3: the edits to draft 26, one row each

"his" marks a source in the ledger; "the lead's" a reading of the notes. What she could have done: a Statement of her own of a K7 kind naming S and the sort, made while V1 is in force (5.6.1(b), 5.6.4), 70%.

Two of three under a named condition: **valid**, **not shown** as in force, the keeper not yet showing it; the way out, Carla's assent too; a fresh version signed by all three beside the valid V2 risks a fork (5.5.4), 80%.

# P5: after wave five: the pin one rule again, on his 22:30, 22:36 and 22:39 and the lead's readings of 22:45

*Added by the scribe's session P5, 27 November 2026, after P3 (P4 wrote nothing). Not a decision, and not part of the hashed text. Nothing above is changed. Marks as M2's: TRACED, COUNTED, INFERRED. The scripts are P5's scratch scripts, outside the repository: `edits.py` holds every edit to `draft-26.md` and `apply.py` applies them to the file as P3 left it (commit `8ac018e3`, sha256 `c770b324…`), asserting each old wording occurs exactly once; `comp.py`, `scen.py`, `comp2.py` do the same for the companion, `rep.py` for the report; `xref.py` and `rq.py` are the checks below. His words are quoted as the ledger on main has them, typos included; in the "Rests on" column a long quotation is shortened with "...". The note is `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-readings-for-p2.md` on main, every line the lead's.*

**The wish** (Nobody, allegedly, 26 November, 16:46): "We need to change the current wish to updating the core after the findings".

**In plain words.** He said that an update the keeper never showed is no update, that a dead keeper leaves the agreement dead and what follows is a new agreement, and that no rule is written for a case the other rules already settle. So the way out by knots, written by P1 and P3, is taken out: where Terms name keepers, the keepers order every act; where none, the knots do. What replaces it is a few sentences: the gate under keepers counts only what they show; an agreement whose keepers fall silent stays where they left it, and its parties go on by a new first act, which may name it; a buyer's filling under it stands as the keepers showed it.

## P5: the stranger

A subagent that had seen neither the ledger nor P5's assignment read `draft-26.md` alone (a copy after P5-1 to P5-26), with two cases.

- **(1) Three known parties, one keeper alive, no update condition; all three sign V2, each after the other; K has not shown it**: not in force; **not shown** as a replacement, never **not valid**, **per keeper**, K named, the reason "a keeper the Terms name does not yet show it"; the order of the signing does not help, knots neither pinning nor ordering in the keeper's place; V1 in force, per keeper (5.5.1(a), 5.5.5(a), (c), (d), 5.5.7(a), (e), 5.8.3, 5.8.4, 5.13.3), 85%.
- **(2) Two of three under a named condition; the keeper falls silent before showing it; all three sign a new first act W1 naming the old agreement, keeper K2**: V2 "is no update of that version; it competes with nothing and makes no fork", **not shown**, per keeper (5.5.1(a), 5.8.3), 70%; V1 stays, per keeper, and no answer says it ended (5.8.3, 5.5.6), 85%; W1 a new agreement, root W1, no update and no fork, in force once K2 shows its assents (§3.1, 5.4.1, 5.8.3, 5.8.4), 80%; no fork anywhere, 85%; the buyer's filling stands as the keeper showed it, nothing reaching it, W1 included (5.10.6, 5.10.2), 80%. It added: W1 cannot name the old root in `agreement`, which a first act does not carry (§4.2, §4.4 case 1).
- **The three sentences it understood least**: 5.5.1(a)'s "an update not shown so is no update of that version" (temporary or for good; and whether a named condition is still asked); 5.5.4(a)'s "no choice of acts enough for L to pass O's gate avoids an act so shown. Each such choice is read whole"; 5.12.2's "signed by all the parties that needed to sign it".
- **Fixed** (P5-27): "while they do not show it", as 5.8.3 already says. **Listed**: whether a named condition's answer is passed on (the lead's question 1, in the report); W1's field (encoding 3, item 14); the other two, as P3 listed them.
